\displaystyle\underbrace{(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*})^{T}\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)})}_{I_{1}}+\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}+\underbrace{\bm{v}^{*T}\big(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})\big)}_{I_{2}}+\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\theta^{*}\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})+\underbrace{\bm{v}^{*T}(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*}))+\theta^{*}\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})}_{I_{3}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S^{(1)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\theta^{*}\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

For
I_{1}
, we know

I_{1}\leq||\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}||\nabla R_{\delta}^{n}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}})||_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\eta_{2}(n)(\sqrt{\log(d)/(n\delta)}+\delta^{\ell}+M_{n}(\theta^{*}+\eta_{1}(n))))=o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta)^{-1/2}).

Following a similar proof of Lemma
S5
, we can show that

I_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\big(||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\eta_{1}(n)\big[\delta^{\ell-1}+\sqrt{\log(d)/(n\delta^{3})}+M_{n}\theta^{*}\big]\big)=o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta)^{-1/2}).

Finally, for
I_{3}
, since by definition

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})=\nabla^{2}_{\theta|\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}),

(S16)

Lemma
S7
implies that
I_{3}=o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta)^{-1/2})
.
Put all pieces together, we have shown that

|\widehat{S}^{(1)}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)})-(S^{(1)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\theta^{*}\nabla^{2}_{\theta|\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}))|=o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta)^{-1/2}).

A similar result will also hold for
\widehat{S}^{(2)}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(1)})
, and thus we conclude

\displaystyle|\widehat{S}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}})-(S(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\theta^{*}\nabla^{2}_{\theta|\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}))|=o_{\mathbb{P}}((n\delta)^{-1/2}).

(S17)

At this point the conclusion of (
S13
) is shown. To show (
S14
), the above formula also implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(|(n\delta)^{1/2}\widehat{S}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}})/\sigma^{*}|\leq t)\leq\mathbb{P}(L(n)\leq(n\delta)^{1/2}S(\bm{\beta}^{*})/\sigma^{*}\leq U(n))+o(1),

(S18)

where
L(n)=-t-q(n)+\widetilde{C}\sigma^{*-1}\nabla^{2}_{\theta|\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})n^{-\phi}(n\delta)^{1/2}
,
U(n)=t+q(n)+\widetilde{C}\sigma^{*-1}\nabla^{2}_{\theta|\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})n^{-\phi}(n\delta)^{1/2}
, and
q(n)=o(1)
is some deterministic sequence. Since
n^{-\phi}(n\delta)^{1/2}\nabla^{2}_{\theta|\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})\sigma^{*-1}\rightarrow\infty
, it is easily seen that
\mathbb{P}(|(n\delta)^{1/2}\widehat{S}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}})/\sigma^{*}|\leq t)\rightarrow 0
. Since
\widehat{\sigma}
is consistent, for
n
large enough we will get
|\widehat{\sigma}/\sigma-1|\leq 3
, which finally implies the desired result.
This completes the proof.

S8.4
Proof of Theorem
4

The proof is very similar to the proof of Theorem
1
so we only give a sketch here. Following the same derivation of Lemma
1
, we can show that

\displaystyle(n\delta)^{1/2}\frac{S_{\delta}^{L}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*},\bm{\omega}^{*}_{L})-\delta^{\ell}\mu^{*}_{L}}{\sigma^{*}_{L}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,1).

(S19)

Next, following the derivation of Theorem
1
, we can show that
I_{1}=(n\delta)^{1/2}|\bm{v}^{*T}_{L}\bm{C}(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{0}^{(2)})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*}))|
and
I_{2}=(n\delta)^{1/2}|(\widehat{\bm{v}}_{L}^{(1)}-\bm{v}_{L}^{*})\bm{C}\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}_{0}^{(2)})|
are
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, which further implies that
(n\delta)^{1/2}|S_{\delta}^{L(1)}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)},\widehat{\bm{\omega}}^{(1)}_{L})-S_{\delta}^{L}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*},\bm{\omega}^{*}_{L})|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. This shall hold similarly for
S_{\delta}^{L(2)}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(1)},\widehat{\bm{\omega}}^{(2)}_{L})
. Invoking Slutsky’s theorem completes the proof.

S8.5
Proofs of additional technical lemmas

Lemma S2
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, for any fixed
\bm{\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle||\nabla R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla R(\bm{\beta})||_{\infty}\lesssim\delta^{\ell},

(S20)

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla R(\bm{\beta})||_{\max}\lesssim\delta^{\ell-1}.

Proof of Lemma
S2
.

We focus on proving the result for
||\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla^{2}R(\bm{\beta})||_{\max}
and the proof for the other one is very similar. By definition

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla^{2}R(\bm{\beta})||_{\max}

(S21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}yz_{j}z_{k}(\int\frac{-1}{\delta^{2}}K^{\prime}(\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta})f(x|y,\bm{z})dx-f^{\prime}(\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z}))f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}yz_{j}z_{k}(\int\frac{-1}{\delta}K^{\prime}(u)f(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})du-f^{\prime}(\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z}))f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}yz_{j}z_{k}\int K(u)(f^{\prime}(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{\prime}(\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z}))duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}yz_{j}z_{k}\int K(u)\frac{(u\delta)^{\ell-1}}{(\ell-1)!}f^{(\ell)}(\tau u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\ell-1}),

where the first equality is by definition, the second equality follows from a change of variable, the third equality follows from an integration by parts, the last equality follows from Assumptions
1
,
2
and the last inequality follows from Assumptions
3
,
4
. The proof is complete.
∎

Lemma S3
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, for any fixed
\bm{\beta}
, we have with probability greater than
1-\mathcal{O}(d^{-1})

\displaystyle||\nabla R^{n}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})||_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta}},

(S22)

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}R^{n}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})||_{\max}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}.

Proof of Lemma
S3
.

We focus on proving the result for
||\nabla^{2}R^{n}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})||_{\max}
and the proof for the other one is very similar. Denote

\displaystyle T_{ijk}=

\displaystyle(\nabla^{2}\bar{R}^{i}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}))_{jk}

(S23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-w(y_{i})y_{i}\frac{z_{ij}z_{ik}}{\delta^{2}}K^{\prime}(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta})-(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}))_{jk}.

By definition, we know
\mathbb{E}[T_{ijk}]=0
. Meanwhile for
Var[T_{ijk}]
, we know

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\nabla^{2}\bar{R}^{i}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}))_{jk}^{2}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\int w(y)^{2}\frac{z_{j}^{2}z_{k}^{2}}{\delta^{4}}K^{\prime 2}(\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta})f(x|y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

(S24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}\int w(y)^{2}\frac{z_{j}^{2}z_{k}^{2}}{\delta^{3}}K^{\prime 2}(u)f(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}),

and with a similar derivation
(\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}))^{2}_{jk}=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\delta^{2}})
. This shows that
Var[T_{ijk}]=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{\delta^{3}})
. Since
|T_{ijk}|\lesssim\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\delta^{2}}
, by Bernstein inequality we can show that with probability greater than
1-\mathcal{O}(d^{-1})

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}R^{n}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta})||_{\max}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}.

(S25)

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S4
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, it holds that

\displaystyle||\nabla R(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})-\nabla R(\bm{\beta}^{*})||_{\infty}\lesssim M_{n}||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1},

(S26)

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}R(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})-\nabla^{2}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})||_{\max}\lesssim M_{n}||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1}.

Proof of Lemma
S4
.

Here we prove the second inequality and the first one should follow similarly. By definition,

\displaystyle||\nabla^{2}R(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})-\nabla^{2}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})||_{\max}

(S27)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}\bigg|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{j}z_{k}\big[f^{\prime}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{\prime}(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})\big]f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1}M_{n}|f^{\prime\prime}|_{\infty}\max_{j,k}\sum_{y}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{j}Z_{k}||Y=y]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{n}||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1}.

∎

Lemma S5
.

Under the conditions in Theorem
1
, we have

(n\delta)^{1/2}\bigg|\bm{v}^{*T}\bigg(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}^{*})\bigg)\bigg|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

for
(j,k)\in\{(1,2),(2,1)\}
.

Proof of Lemma
S5
.

With some algebra we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\bm{v}^{*T}\bigg(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\nabla^{2}R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}^{*})\bigg)\bigg|

(S28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{\bm{z}_{i}^{T}\bm{v}^{*}y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\bigg|\bigg|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{\bm{z}_{i}y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg|\bigg|_{\infty}.

Now we start to analyze
\bigg|\bigg|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{\bm{z}_{i}y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg|\bigg|_{\infty}
. Denote
G_{i}=w(y_{i})\frac{\bm{z}_{i}y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt
and
G_{im},1\leq m\leq d
as its coordinates.
Consider the event
A:=\{||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1}\lesssim C\eta_{1}(n)\}
for some constant
C
. Notice that for each
m

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}y}{\delta}\int_{x}\int_{\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}}{\delta}-t)dtf(x|y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

(S29)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(u=(x-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z})/\delta)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{m}y\int_{u}\int_{u}^{u+\triangle}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(u+\triangle-t)dtf(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{m}y\int_{u}\int_{u}^{u+\triangle}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(u+\triangle-t)dtf(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle+\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{m}y\int_{u}u\delta\int_{u}^{u+\triangle}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(u+\triangle-t)dtf^{\prime}(\tau u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z},

where
\triangle=\frac{(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta})^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta}
and
\tau\in[0,1]
. Here the first step is by definition and the last step is from the mean value theorem. Since

\displaystyle\int_{u}\int_{u}^{u+\triangle}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(u+\triangle-t)dtdu=0,

(S30)

we can show that the first term on the RHS of the last step is 0. The second term on the RHS can be bounded by
C^{\prime}\delta|\triangle|^{2}\lesssim M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}/\delta
for some constant
C^{\prime}
on event
A
. This implies that
\mathbb{E}[G_{im}|A]\lesssim M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}/\delta
.

Now we look at its variance. For the second moment, for each
i\in\mathcal{N}_{j},m=1,\dotso,d
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(w(y_{i})\frac{z_{im}y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg)^{2}\bigg|A\bigg]

(S31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{y}w(y)^{2}\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}^{2}}{\delta}\int_{u}\bigg(\int_{u}^{u+\triangle}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(u+\triangle-t)dt\bigg)^{2}f(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}|A\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{y}w(y)^{2}\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}^{2}}{\delta}2|f|_{\infty}|K^{\prime\prime}|^{2}_{\infty}\triangle^{4}f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}|A\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{M^{4}_{n}\eta_{1}(n)^{4}}{\delta^{5}}.

Also we know for each
i\in\mathcal{N}_{j},m=1,\dotso,d
,
\bigg|\frac{z_{im}y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg|\lesssim\frac{M_{n}}{\delta}(\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta})^{2}
on
A
, and therefore

\displaystyle Var[G_{im}|A]\lesssim\frac{M^{4}_{n}\eta_{1}(n)^{4}}{\delta^{5}}.

(S32)

Therefore, applying Bernstein inequality with
M_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{m}|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{m}|>\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}~\big|A\bigg)\leq\mathcal{O}(d^{-1}),

(S33)

which further implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{m}|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{m}|>\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}\bigg)

(S34)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{m}|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{m}|>\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}~\big|A\bigg)+\mathbb{P}(A^{C})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(1),

where the last step follows from Assumption
6
.

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(Y_{i})\frac{\bm{Z}_{i}Y_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{X_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{Z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{X_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\bm{Z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{X_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\bm{Z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg|\bigg|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta}).

(S35)

Under the conditions of Theorem
1
, the desired result holds. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S6
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1
,
let
s^{\prime}=||\bm{\omega}^{*}||_{1}
and
\xi>0
is some constant. If
s^{\prime}\big(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}\vee\delta^{\ell-1}\big)=o(1)
, then with probability tending to one it holds that
\kappa_{D}(s^{\prime})\geq\kappa/\sqrt{2}
, where

\kappa_{D}(s^{\prime})=\min\bigg\{\frac{s^{\prime 1/2}(\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})\bm{v})^{1/2}}{||\bm{v}_{s^{\prime}}||_{1}}:\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\backslash\{0\},||\bm{v}_{s^{\prime c}}||_{1}\leq\xi||\bm{v}_{s^{\prime}}||_{1}\bigg\}.

Proof.

This proof is similar to the proof of Lemma J.3 in
Ning and Liu 2017
so we only give a sketch here. Firstly we have

\displaystyle\kappa_{D}(s^{\prime})^{2}\geq\min\bigg\{\frac{\bm{v}^{T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})\bm{v}}{||\bm{v}||_{2}^{2}}:\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}\backslash\{0\},||\bm{v}_{s^{\prime c}}||_{1}\leq\xi||\bm{v}_{s^{\prime}}||_{1}\bigg\}.

(S36)

Similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, Lemmas
S2
,
S3
,
S4
and
S11
together imply that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}})-\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}))\bm{v}}{||\bm{v}||_{2}^{2}}\bigg|

(S37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\bm{v}^{T}\big[\frac{1}{2}\big(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})+\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n_{(2)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)})\big)-\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})\big]\bm{v}}{||\bm{v}||_{2}^{2}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle s^{\prime}(\xi+1)^{2}\big[M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}\vee\delta^{\ell-1}\big]=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where the inequality is because
||\bm{v}||_{1}^{2}\leq s^{\prime}(\xi+1)^{2}||\bm{v}||_{2}^{2}
.
Therefore for
n
large enough, we have

|\frac{\bm{v}^{T}(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n}_{\delta}(\widetilde{\bm{\beta}})-\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}))\bm{v}}{||\bm{v}||_{2}^{2}}|\leq\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{2}
. This implies
\kappa_{D}(\widetilde{s}^{*})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\kappa^{2}
with probability tending to 1. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S7
.

Under the same conditions as in Theorem
2
, for
j=1,2
, it holds that

(n\delta)^{1/2}|\bm{v}^{*T}(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*}))+\theta^{*}\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Proof.

By definition

\displaystyle|\bm{v}^{*T}(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*}))+\theta^{*}\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})|

(S38)

\displaystyle\leq||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\bigg[\underbrace{||\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})+\theta^{*}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R^{n_{(j)}}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*})||_{\infty}}_{I_{1}}+

\displaystyle\underbrace{||\theta^{*}(\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R^{n_{(j)}}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*}))||_{\infty}}_{I_{2}}+\underbrace{||\theta^{*}(\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}))||_{\infty}}_{I_{3}}\bigg].

For
I_{1}
, similar to Lemma
S5
, we can write

\displaystyle|\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*})+\theta^{*}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R^{n_{(j)}}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*})|

(S39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{0}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{0}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\bigg|

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}G_{i}|,

where
G_{i}=w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{0}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}_{0}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t)dt\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Thus, similar to the derivation of Lemma
S5
, for each
1\leq m\leq d
, we can show that

\mathbb{E}[G_{im}]\lesssim M_{n}^{2}\theta^{*2}/\delta
,
|G_{im}|\lesssim M_{n}^{3}\theta^{*2}/\delta^{3}
and
Var[G_{im}]\lesssim M_{n}^{4}\theta^{*4}/\delta^{5}
, and thus applying Bernstein inequality yields

I_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{M_{n}^{2}\theta^{*2}}{\delta}).

Meanwhile, Lemma
S3
and Lemma
S2
imply that
I_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\theta^{*}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}})
and
I_{3}=\mathcal{O}(\theta^{*}\delta^{\ell-1})
. Combing the above results, we obtain

\displaystyle|\bm{v}^{*T}(\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(0,\bm{\gamma}^{*})-\nabla R_{\delta}^{n_{(j)}}(\bm{\beta}^{*}))+\theta^{*}\bm{v}^{*T}\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\theta}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\frac{||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\theta^{*}}{\delta}(M_{n}^{2}\theta^{*}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta}}\vee\delta^{\ell})).

(S40)

This together with the condition in Theorem
2
completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S8
.

Let
s^{\prime}=||\bm{\omega}^{*}||_{0}
. Suppose Assumptions
1
-
5
hold, and
\lambda_{\min}(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma},\bm{\gamma}}R(\bm{\beta}^{*}))\geq c
for some constant
c>0
.
If we choose
\delta\asymp(\log(d)/n)^{1/(2\ell+1)}
and tuning parameter
\lambda^{\prime}\asymp||\bm{\omega}^{*}||_{1}(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)+(\log(d)/n)^{(\ell-1)/(2\ell+1)})
and
s^{\prime}(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)+(\log(d)/n)^{(\ell-1)/(2\ell+1)})=o(1)
, then it holds that

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\omega}}-\bm{\omega}^{*}||_{1}\lesssim||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}s^{\prime}(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)+(\log(d)/n)^{(\ell-1)/(2\ell+1)}).

(S41)

Proof.

Here we focus on the rate for
\widehat{\bm{\omega}}^{(1)}
and the result will follow accordingly.
Denote
\widehat{\triangle}=\widehat{\bm{\omega}}^{(1)}-\bm{\omega}^{*}
. By definition, we can show that
||\widehat{\triangle}_{\widetilde{s}^{*c}}||_{1}\leq||\widehat{\triangle}_{\widetilde{s}^{*}}||_{1}
.

With some algebra, we will get

\displaystyle\widehat{\triangle}^{T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\widehat{\triangle}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\triangle}^{T}(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\cdot}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\bm{v}^{*})-\widehat{\triangle}^{T}(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\cdot}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)})

(S42)

\displaystyle\leq||\widehat{\triangle}||_{1}||\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\cdot}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\bm{v}^{*}||_{\infty}+||\widehat{\triangle}||_{1}||\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\theta}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\widehat{\bm{\omega}}^{(1)}||_{\infty}.

By definition, the second term is bounded by
\lambda^{\prime}||\widehat{\triangle}||_{1}
. For the first term, recall by definition
\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\cdot}R(\bm{\beta}^{*})\bm{v}^{*}=0
, and thus similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, Lemma
S2
,
S3
and
S4
imply that with probability approaching to 1

||\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\cdot}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\bm{v}^{*}||_{\infty}\lesssim||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\bigg[M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}\vee\delta^{\ell-1}\bigg],

and thus with the choice of
\lambda^{\prime}
it holds that
\widehat{\triangle}^{T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{\gamma}\bm{\gamma}}R^{n_{(1)}}_{\delta}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\widehat{\triangle}\lesssim\lambda^{\prime}||\widehat{\triangle}||_{1}
. This together with Lemma
S6
implies that
||\widehat{\triangle}||_{1}\lesssim s^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime}
with high probability. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S9
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
1
, if
U
is a proper kernel of order
\ell
satisfying the same condition as
K
in Assumption
2
, and in addition
U
is
\ell
times continuously differentiable and
U^{(i)}
degenerates at the boundary for
i=0,\dotso,\ell-1
, then when
\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{nh^{2\ell+1}}}+(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\vee h)^{\zeta}=o(1)
, it holds that

|\widehat{\mu}-\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{b}^{*}|\lesssim||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}\bigg(\eta_{2}(n)+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{nh^{2\ell+1}}}+(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\vee h)^{\zeta}\bigg).

Proof.

It suffices to proof the results for
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})
. By definition,

\displaystyle|\bm{v}^{*T}T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})|\leq|(\bm{v}^{*}-\widehat{\bm{v}})^{T}\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})|+|\bm{v}^{*T}(T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}))|.

(S43)

We firstly look at
||T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}
. Direct calculation gives that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle||T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}

(S44)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-T^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}+||T^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widetilde{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}+||\widetilde{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty},

where
\widetilde{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}):=\sum_{y\in\{-1,1\}}w(y)y\int\frac{\bm{z}}{h^{1+\ell}}\int U^{(\ell)}\bigg(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}-x}{h}\bigg)f(x|y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}
. For the first term, we have

\displaystyle||T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-T^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}

(S45)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\sum_{y\in\{-1,1\}}w(y)\int y\bm{z}(f^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z}))f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg|\bigg|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}((M_{n}\eta_{1}(n))^{\zeta}),

where the last step follows from Assumption
1
. For the second term, notice that we can rewrite

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})

(S46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\{-1,1\}}w(y)y\int\frac{\bm{z}}{h}\int U\bigg(\frac{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}-x}{h}\bigg)f^{(\ell)}(x|y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\{-1,1\}}w(y)y\int\bm{z}\int U(u)f^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}-hu|y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z},

where the first step is by repeated integration by parts and the second step is by a change of variable. This implies that

\displaystyle||T^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-\widetilde{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}

(S47)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg|\sum_{y\in\{-1,1\}}w(y)y\int\bm{z}\int U(u)\bigg(f^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{(\ell)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}-hu|y,\bm{z})\bigg)duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg|\bigg|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(h^{\zeta}).

Finally, similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, we can show that the third term on the RHS of (
S44
) is
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{nh^{2\ell+1}}})
based on Lemmas
S3
and
S11
. Putting all three terms together we have

\displaystyle||T^{(\ell)}(\bm{\beta}^{*})-\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{nh^{2\ell+1}}}+(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\vee h)^{\zeta}\bigg),

(S48)

and thus
||\widehat{T}_{h,U}^{(\ell),n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by the condition of this lemma. Plugging this back to (
S43
) gives the desired result. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S10
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
1
, if
L
is a proper kernel of order
\ell
satisfying Assumption
2
and
g^{\ell}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{ng}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)=o(1)
, then we have

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}\bm{v}^{*}|\lesssim||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\bigg(\eta_{2}(n)+g^{\ell}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{ng}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\bigg).

(S49)

Proof.

It suffices to show the convergence rate of
\widetilde{\mu}_{K}\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{n_{(1)}}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}
.
Similar to the proof of Theorem
1
, following Lemmas
S2
,
S3
,
S4
and
S11
, we can show that

\displaystyle||\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{(1)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-H(\bm{\beta}^{*})||_{\max}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(g^{\ell}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{ng}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\bigg).

(S50)

Since
||H(\bm{\beta}^{*})||_{\max}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we know
||\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{(1)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\max}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
when
g^{\ell}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{ng}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)=o(1)
.
Now by triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle

\displaystyle|\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{(1)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*T}H(\bm{\beta}^{*})\bm{v}^{*}|

(S51)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*}||^{2}_{1}||\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{(1)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\max}+2||\bm{v}^{*T}\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{(1)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})||_{\infty}||\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}+|\bm{v}^{*T}(\widehat{H}_{g,K}^{(1)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})-H(\bm{\beta}^{*}))\bm{v}^{*}|.

This implies that

\displaystyle|\widehat{\sigma}^{2}-\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}\bm{v}^{*}|=||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\eta_{2}(n)+g^{\ell}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{ng}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\bigg).

(S52)

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S11

(Lemma 6.1 of
Chernozhukov et al. 2018
)
.

Let
\{\bm{X}_{m}\}
and
\{\bm{Y}_{m}\}
be sequences of random vectors. If
||\bm{X}_{m}||=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(A_{m})
conditional on
\{\bm{Y}_{m}\}
for a sequence of positive constants
\{A_{m}\}
, then
||\bm{X}_{m}||=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(A_{m})
unconditionally.

S9
Theoretical Results and Discussions for the Bandwidth Selection

S9.1
Key results

In this section, we collect a few key results in the analysis of bandwidth selection procedures and defer the proofs to Section
S9.2
.

Lemma S12
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
6
, if
\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee d)}{n\delta}}\vee M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)=o(1)
, and
M_{n}^{2}\sqrt{\log(n\vee d)/n^{\epsilon_{1}}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\widehat{V}(\delta)-V(\delta)|\lesssim\frac{\psi_{1}(n,\delta)}{\delta},

(S1)

uniformly over all
\delta\in\Delta
, where

\displaystyle\psi_{1}(n,\delta)=||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\bigg(\eta_{2}(n)\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee d)}{n\delta}}\vee M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\bigg).

(S2)

Lemma S13
.

Suppose Assumptions
1
-
7
hold. Choose
J
as a proper kernel function of order
r
satisfying the conditions of
K
in Assumption
2
. In addition, assume
J
is
\ell
times continuously differentiable and
J^{(i)}
degenerates at the boundary for
i=1,\dotso,\ell-1
. If
M_{n}\leq C\sqrt{nb/\log(nd)}
for some constant
C
and
\frac{\log(n\vee d)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}=o(1)
, then it holds that

\displaystyle|\widehat{SB}(\delta)-SB(\delta)|\lesssim\delta^{2\ell}\psi_{2}(n,\delta)

(S3)

uniformly over all
\delta\in\Delta
, where

\displaystyle\psi_{2}(n,\delta)=||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee d)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}}\vee(\delta\vee b)^{r}\vee M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)(1\vee\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta^{\ell}})\vee\eta_{2}(n)\bigg).

(S4)

In view of (
S3
) and (
S4
), the error terms
\sqrt{\frac{\log(n\vee d)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}}
and
(\delta\vee b)^{r}
correspond to the variance and bias when estimating
A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)
in (
4.4
) with the extra smoothing step. This rate is similar to the case of estimating the
\ell
th derivative of a density function using a kernel of order
r
in the context of kernel density estimation under the smoothness Assumption
7
. The terms
M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)(1\vee\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta^{\ell}})
and
\eta_{2}(n)
come from the plug-in error of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
and
\widehat{\bm{v}}
, respectively.

Now we are ready to present the following theorem on the rate of
\widehat{\delta}
.

Theorem S6
.

Under the conditions in Lemmas
S12
and
S13
, if
\psi_{1}(n,\delta),\psi_{2}(n,\delta)=o(1)
, then with probability tending to 1,

\frac{\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}}{\delta^{*}}\lesssim\psi_{1}(n,\delta^{*})\vee\psi_{2}(n,\delta^{*}).

Thus,
\widehat{\delta}/\delta^{*}\rightarrow 1
in probability.

Remark S3
.

Notice that in Theorem
S6
, the MSE-optimal bandwidth satisfies
\delta^{*}\asymp n^{-1/(2\ell+1)}
. To examine the order of
\psi_{1}(n,\delta^{*})\vee\psi_{2}(n,\delta^{*})
, we consider
d\gtrsim n
(high-dimensional case) and optimize the bandwidth
b
in
\psi_{2}(n,\delta^{*})
, leading to
b\asymp(\log d/n)^{1/(2\ell+2r+1)}
.
Furthermore, take
\eta_{1}(n),\eta_{2}(n)
as the rate from
Feng et al. 2022
and Lemma
S8
, and assume that
||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1},M_{n}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then
\psi_{1}(n,\delta^{*})\vee\psi_{2}(n,\delta^{*})
can be simplified to

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\log d}{n}\Big)^{r/(2\ell+2r+1)}\vee s^{(4\ell+1)/(2\ell+1)}\Big(\frac{\log^{2}d}{n}\Big)^{\ell/(2\ell+1)}\vee s^{\prime}\Big(\frac{\log d}{n}\Big)^{(\ell-1)/(2\ell+1)}.

(S5)

There are in general two scenarios: firstly, if
r
is small relative to
\ell
while
s,s^{\prime}
do not grow too fast, then
(\log d/n)^{r/(2\ell+2r+1)}
from the extra smoothing step for estimating
SB(\delta)
will be the dominant term. However, if
r
is relatively large such that
r/(2\ell+2r+1)\geq(\ell-1)/(2\ell+1)
, then the last two terms in (
S5
) due to
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\omega}}
dominate. In this case, even if
f(x|y,\bm{z})
has a large amount of extra smoothness
r
, the convergence rate of
\widehat{\delta}
cannot be further improved, as the rate is dominated by the error from
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}
and
\widehat{\bm{\omega}}
.

Recall that our goal is to show the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{U}_{n}(\widehat{\delta})
. We need the following additional assumption on the kernel function.

Assumption S8
.

Assume
K^{\prime\prime}(\cdot),K^{\prime\prime\prime}(\cdot)<C_{1}
for some constant
C_{1}
,
K^{\prime}
degenerates at the boundaries, and

\int|K(u)|du,\int|u||K(u)|du,

\int|u|^{p}|K^{\prime}(u)|du,\forall p\in\{0,1,2,3\},

\int|u|^{q}|K^{\prime\prime}(u)|du,\forall q\in\{2,3,4,5\},

\int|u|^{t}|K^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)|du,\forall t\in\{1,2,3\},

are all bounded by some constant.

Similar to Section
2.3
, we need to use the cross-fitting approach to estimate
\delta
. Specifically, we split the data into 3 equal folds. Following the approach described in Section
2.3
, we construct
\widehat{S}^{(1)}_{\delta}(\theta,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)})
and
\widehat{S}^{(2)}_{\delta}(\theta,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(1)})
using the first and second folds. We further estimate the variance and bias in the same way as in Section
4
, and denote the estimators by
\widehat{V}^{(1,2)}(\delta)
and
\widehat{B}^{(1,2)}(\delta)
, where the superscript
(1,2)
refers to the fact that we only use the data in the first and second folds. Then we estimate
\delta
by

\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\delta}\Big[\frac{1}{n}\widehat{V}^{(1,2)}(\delta)+\frac{n-1}{n}(\widehat{B}^{(1,2)}(\delta))^{2}\Big].

Finally, we plug-in
\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)}
into the third fold of the data to construct the bias corrected smoothed decorrelated score statistic

\widehat{U}_{n}^{(3)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)})=\sqrt{n_{3}\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)}}\Big(\frac{\widehat{S}^{(3)}_{\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)}}(0,\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(1,2)})-(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)})^{\ell}\widehat{\mu}^{(1,2)}}{\widehat{\sigma}^{(1,2)}}\Big),

where
n_{3}
is the sample size for the third fold,
\widehat{\mu}^{(1,2)}
and
\widehat{\sigma}^{(1,2)}
are the cross-fitted estimators in (
2.16
),
\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(1,2)}=(\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(1)}+\widehat{\bm{\gamma}}^{(2)})/2
, and

\widehat{S}^{(3)}_{\delta}(\theta,\bm{\gamma})=\nabla_{\theta}R^{(3)}_{\delta}(\theta,\bm{\gamma})-\widehat{\bm{\omega}}^{(1,2)T}\nabla_{\bm{\gamma}}R^{(3)}_{\delta}(\theta,\bm{\gamma}),

in which we use the data in the third fold to compute the gradient
\nabla_{\theta}R^{(3)}_{\delta}(\theta,\bm{\gamma})
and
\nabla_{\bm{\gamma}}R^{(3)}_{\delta}(\theta,\bm{\gamma})
. Similarly, we can construct
\widehat{U}_{n}^{(1)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(2,3)})
and
\widehat{U}_{n}^{(2)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,3)})
. The following theorem indeed implies that
\widehat{U}_{n}^{(3)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,1)
, and
\widehat{U}_{n}^{(1)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(2,3)})
,
\widehat{U}_{n}^{(2)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,3)})
and
\widehat{U}_{n}^{(3)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)})
are asymptotically independent. Thus, the final test statistic given by
[\widehat{U}_{n}^{(1)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(2,3)})+\widehat{U}_{n}^{(2)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,3)})+\widehat{U}_{n}^{(3)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)})]/\sqrt{3}
is asymptotically
N(0,1)
.

Theorem S7
.

Under the conditions in Lemmas
S12
and
S13
, Theorem
1
and Assumption
S8
, we further assume
\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|X_{i}|\leq M_{n}
and
\|\bm{\beta}^{*}\|_{1}
is bounded by a constant.
If

\frac{M_{n}^{3}}{\delta^{*2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n\delta^{*}}}=\mathcal{O}(1),

(S6)

and

(n\delta^{*})^{1/2}\left\|\bm{v}^{*}\right\|_{1}C_{n,\delta^{*}}\left(C_{n,\delta^{*}}\vee\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{*}}\vee\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n\delta^{*}}}\vee\delta^{*(\ell-1)}\eta_{1}(n)\vee\delta^{*\ell}\eta_{2}(n)\right)=o(1),

(S7)

where
\psi_{1}\left(n,\delta^{*}\right)\vee\psi_{2}\left(n,\delta^{*}\right)\coloneqq C_{n,\delta^{*}}=o(1)
, then under
H_{0}:\theta^{*}=0
, it holds that

\widehat{U}_{n}^{(3)}(\widehat{\delta}^{(1,2)})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\rightarrow}}N(0,1).

This is a more complete version of Theorem
3
in the main paper. In the next subsection, we provide the proof of Theorem
S7
.

Remark S4
.

As remarked after Theorem
S6
, take
\eta_{1}(n),\eta_{2}(n)
as the rate from
Feng et al. 2022
and Lemma
S8
, and assume that
\left\|\bm{v}^{*}\right\|_{1},M_{n}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, then we have

C_{n,\delta^{*}}\asymp\left(\frac{\log d}{n}\right)^{r/(2\ell+2r+1)}\vee s^{(4\ell+1)/(2\ell+1)}\left(\frac{\log^{2}d}{n}\right)^{\ell/(2\ell+1)}\vee s^{\prime}\left(\frac{\log d}{n}\right)^{(\ell-1)/(2\ell+1)}.

(S8)

Since the optimal bandwidth has
\delta^{*}\asymp n^{-1/(2\ell+1)}
, (
S6
) is satisfied if we have
\ell\geq 3
and
\log d\ll n^{\frac{2(\ell-2)}{2\ell+1}}
. Given (
S8
), with some calculation we can show that (
S7
) reduces to

(n\delta^{*})^{1/2}C_{n,\delta^{*}}^{2}=o(1).

Followed by the remark after Theorem
S6
, if the first term in (
S8
) dominates, to satisfy the condition above we need
r>\frac{\ell(2\ell+1)}{2(\ell+1)}
(ignoring the
\log d
factor for simplicity). If the last two terms in (
S8
) dominate and assume
s,s^{\prime}=O(1)
, then
\ell\geq 3
is sufficient for the condition above.

S9.2
Proofs

S9.2.1
Proof of Lemma
S12

It suffices to show the rate for
\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}
.
Let
\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\nabla\bar{R}_{\delta}^{1}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\nabla\bar{R}_{\delta}^{1}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})^{T}\bigg]
, and
\bm{\Gamma}(\delta)=\mathbb{E}[\nabla\bar{R}^{1}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*})\nabla\bar{R}^{1}_{\delta}(\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}]
. We firstly look at

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\bm{\Gamma}(\delta)||_{\max}\leq||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)||_{\max}+||\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)-\bm{\Gamma}(\delta)||_{\max}.

(S9)

Now we bound the first term. For any
\delta\in\Delta
, we can always find a
\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}
such that
|\delta-\widetilde{\delta}|\lesssim n^{-\rho}
, where
\rho
is some constant that can be arbitrarily large and the set
\widetilde{\Delta}
that is a subset of
\Delta
has cardinality of order
\mathcal{O}(n^{\rho})
. For such pair
(\delta,\widetilde{\delta})
, we can show that

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sqrt{n\delta^{3}}||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)||_{\max}

(S10)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}\bigg(||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}+||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}+||\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)||_{\max}\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}+\mathcal{O}(n^{-1/2}),

where the first inequality is by taking
\rho
large enough.
To see last step of above, recall that under Assumptions
2
,
3
and
4

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}=

\displaystyle\max_{j,k}\bigg|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}w(y_{i})^{2}\bm{z}_{ij}\bm{z}_{ik}\bigg[\frac{K^{2}((x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}_{i})/\delta)}{\delta^{2}}-\frac{K^{2}((x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}_{i})/\widetilde{\delta})}{\widetilde{\delta}^{2}}\bigg]\bigg|

(S11)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{n}^{2}(\frac{1}{\delta^{2}}-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\delta}^{2}}).

Thus, taking
\rho
large enough will ensure
\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}(1+o(1))||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-1/2})
. With a similar derivation we will also obtain
\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}(1+o(1))||\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-1/2})
, and thus (
S10
) holds.

Now we start to bound
\max_{\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}}\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}
.
Since

|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}_{ijk}(\widetilde{\delta})|=|(\nabla\bar{R}_{\widetilde{\delta}}^{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\nabla\bar{R}_{\widetilde{\delta}}^{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})^{T})_{jk}|\lesssim\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\widetilde{\delta}^{2}}

and

Var[(\nabla\bar{R}_{\widetilde{\delta}}^{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\nabla\bar{R}_{\widetilde{\delta}}^{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})^{T})_{jk}]\lesssim\frac{1}{\widetilde{\delta}^{3}},

by applying Bernstein inequality similar to the proof of Lemma
S3
, conditioned on
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
we can show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}(\max_{\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}}\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}>t)

(S12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{k=1}^{d}\mathbb{P}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}(\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}|\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})|_{jk}>t)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle n^{\rho}d^{2}\exp\bigg(-\frac{\frac{1}{2}t^{2}/\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}{\frac{c_{0}}{\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}+\frac{c1M_{n}^{2}t}{3\widetilde{\delta}^{3}\sqrt{n\widetilde{\delta}}}}\bigg),

where
c_{0},c_{1}>0
are some constants. By taking
t=c_{2}\sqrt{\log(nd)}
for some constant
c_{2}
large enough, we obtain that conditioned on
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
, with probability greater than
1-\mathcal{O}((nd)^{-1})

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\widetilde{\delta})||_{\max}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{n\widetilde{\delta}^{3}}}

(S13)

uniformly over
\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}
.
This combines with Lemma
S11
further implies that with probability approaching to 1

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)||_{\max}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{n\delta^{3}}}

(S14)

uniformly over
\delta\in\Delta
.

For the second term on the RHS of (
S9
), similar to Lemma
S4
, we can show that

\displaystyle||\widetilde{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)-\bm{\Gamma}(\delta)||_{\max}\lesssim\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta}.

(S15)

This implies that

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)-\bm{\Gamma}(\delta)||_{\max}=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{n\delta}}\vee M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)).

(S16)

Meanwhile, since

\displaystyle||\delta\cdot\bm{\Gamma}(\delta)||_{\max}=

\displaystyle\max_{i,j}\bigg|\sum_{y}w(y)^{2}\int\frac{z_{i}z_{j}}{\delta}\int K^{2}(\frac{y(x-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z})}{\delta})f(x|y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg|

(S17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(1),

under the condition that
\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{n\delta}}\vee M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)=o(1)
, we know
||\delta\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)||_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Finally, by triangle inequality

\displaystyle|\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*T}{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)\bm{v}^{*}|

(S18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle||\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}||\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v})||_{\infty}+2||\bm{v}^{*T}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)||_{\infty}||\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}+|\bm{v}^{*T}(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)-{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta))\bm{v}^{*}|,

which further implies that with probability approaching 1

|\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}^{(1)}(\delta)\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)}-\bm{v}^{*T}{\bm{\Gamma}}(\delta)\bm{v}^{*}|\lesssim\frac{||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}}{\delta}\bigg(\eta_{2}(n)+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{n\delta}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\bigg)

uniformly over
\delta\in\Delta
. This completes the proof.

S9.2.2
Proof of Lemma
S13

It suffices to bound
\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\bigg)^{2}-SB(\delta)\bigg|
. Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}(\bm{\beta},\delta)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\bm{\beta}}(A_{1}(\bm{\beta},\delta))

(S19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)y\int_{\bm{z}}\bm{z}\int_{t}J(t)\int_{u}K(u)[f(u\delta+tb+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(tb+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})]dudtf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}.

By definition,

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\bigg)^{2}-SB(\delta)\bigg|

(S20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\bigg)^{2}-\big(\bm{v}^{*T}A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)\big)^{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\bigg)^{2}-\big(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)^{2}\bigg|+\bigg|\big(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)^{2}-\big(\bm{v}^{*T}A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)\big)^{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)\bigg)^{2}\bigg|}_{I_{1}}

\displaystyle+2\underbrace{\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\cdot\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\big(\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)\bigg|}_{I_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\bigg|\big(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)^{2}-\big(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)^{2}\bigg|}_{I_{3}}+\underbrace{\bigg|\big(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)^{2}-\big(\bm{v}^{*T}A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)\big)^{2}\bigg|}_{I_{4}}.

Now we start to bound each term.

•

For
I_{1}
,
we firstly study
\delta^{-\ell}||\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}
condition on
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma
S10
, it suffices to bound

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{-\ell}||\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})||_{\infty}

(S21)

uniformly over
\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}
, where
\widetilde{\Delta}
is a subset of
\Delta
with cardinality of order
\mathcal{O}(n^{\rho})
for some constant
\rho>0
, such that for each
\delta\in\Delta
, there exist
\widetilde{\delta}\in\widetilde{\Delta}
with
|\delta-\widetilde{\delta}|\lesssim n^{-\rho}
.

Let
T_{ij}(\widetilde{\delta})=\widetilde{\delta}^{-\ell}\big(A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})-\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})\big)_{j}
, we know
\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}T_{ij}(\widetilde{\delta})=0
. Moreover for all
j
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{-\ell}|(A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta}))_{j}|=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{-\ell}\bigg|w(y_{i})\frac{z_{ij}y_{i}}{b}\int K(u)\big[J(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}_{i}-u\widetilde{\delta}}{b})-J(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{b})\big]\biggr\}du\bigg|

(S22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{-\ell}\bigg|w(y_{i})\frac{z_{ij}y_{i}}{b}\int K(u)\frac{\widetilde{\delta}^{\ell}}{b^{\ell}}\frac{u^{\ell}}{\ell!}J^{\ell}(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}_{i}-\tau u\widetilde{\delta}}{b})\big]\biggr\}du\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{M_{n}}{b^{\ell+1}},

where
\tau\in[0,1]
and the second equality is because
K
is of order
\ell
and mean value theorem. This implies that
|T_{ij}(\widetilde{\delta})|\lesssim\frac{M_{n}}{b^{\ell+1}}
. Now we look at
\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}[T^{2}_{ij}(\widetilde{\delta})]
.

Notice that for all
j

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})_{j}=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\bm{z}_{j}y\int_{t}J(t)\int_{u}K(u)[f(u\widetilde{\delta}+tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})]

(S23)

\displaystyle dudtf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\bm{z}_{j}y\int_{t}J(t)\int_{u}K(u)\frac{(u\widetilde{\delta})^{\ell}}{\ell!}f^{(\ell)}(\tau u\widetilde{\delta}+tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})dudtf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{\ell},

where the second equality is because
K
is of order
\ell
and
\tau\in[0,1]
.
For the second moment, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}[(\widetilde{\delta}^{-\ell}A_{1}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta}))_{j}^{2}]

(S24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{-2\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}\bigg[w(y)^{2}\frac{z_{j}^{2}}{b^{2}}\bigg(\int K(u)\big[J(\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}-u\widetilde{\delta}}{b})-J(\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}}{b})\big]\biggr\}du\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\delta}^{-2\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}\bigg[w(y)^{2}\frac{z_{j}^{2}}{b^{2}}\bigg(\int K(u)\frac{\widetilde{\delta}^{\ell}}{b^{\ell}}\frac{u^{\ell}}{\ell!}J^{\ell}(\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}-\tau u\widetilde{\delta}}{b})du\bigg)^{2}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b^{2\ell+1}}\bigg|\sum_{y}\int_{z}w(y)^{2}z_{j}^{2}\int_{t}\bigg(\int K(u)\frac{u^{\ell}}{\ell!}J^{\ell}(t-\frac{\tau u\widetilde{\delta}}{b})du\bigg)^{2}f(tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})dtf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b^{2\ell+1}},

where the last equality is due to a change of variable
t=\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}}{b}
.
Since
b\rightarrow 0
, we conclude that
\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}[T^{2}_{ij}(\widetilde{\delta})]\lesssim\frac{1}{b^{2\ell+1}}.
Now applying Bernstein inequality similar to Lemma
S10
with
M_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{nb}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, second moment of order
\frac{1}{b^{2\ell+1}}
and each term is bounded by
\frac{M_{n}}{b^{\ell+1}}
, we will obtain that with probability greater than
1-\mathcal{O}((nd)^{-1})
, conditioned on
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}

\displaystyle||\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})-\mathbb{E}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\widetilde{\delta})||_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\widetilde{\delta}^{\ell}}{b^{\ell}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{nb}},

(S25)

which together with Lemma
S11
implies that with probability approaching 1

\displaystyle||\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\delta^{\ell}}{b^{\ell}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{nb}}

(S26)

holds uniformly for all
\delta\in\Delta
.

Therefore we conclude that

\displaystyle I_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\frac{\delta^{2\ell}}{b^{2\ell}}\frac{\log(nd)}{nb}).

(S27)

•

For
I_{2}
, (
S23
) implies that

||\mathbb{E}_{\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}A_{1}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}\lesssim\delta^{\ell}
regardless the choice of
\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
. This combined with (
S26
) further implies that

\displaystyle I_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\delta^{\ell}\sqrt{\frac{\delta^{2\ell}}{b^{2\ell}}\frac{\log(nd)}{nb}})=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\frac{\delta^{2\ell}}{b^{\ell}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{nb}}).

(S28)

•

For
I_{3}
, by definition for all
j
we can write

\displaystyle\big(\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\big)_{j}

(S29)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\bm{z}_{j}y\int_{t}J(t)\int_{u}K(u)[(f(u\delta+tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z}))

\displaystyle-(f(u\delta+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z}))]dudtf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\bm{z}_{j}y\int_{u}\sum_{j=\ell}^{\ell+r-1}\frac{(u\delta)^{j}}{j!}K(u)\int_{t}J(t)[f^{(j)}(tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{(j)}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})]

\displaystyle dtduf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\ell+r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\bm{z}_{j}y\int_{u}\sum_{j=\ell}^{\ell+r-1}\frac{(u\delta)^{j}}{j!}K(u)\int_{t}J(t)\frac{(tb)^{\ell+r-j}}{(\ell+r-j)!}f^{(\ell+r)}(\tau tb+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})

\displaystyle dtduf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}+\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\ell+r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\ell}(\delta\vee b)^{r}),

where
\tau\in[0,1]
.
Here the first equality is by definition, the second equality uses the property that
\int K(u)u^{i}du=0~\forall~i<\ell
, and the third equality uses the property that
\int J(u)u^{i}du=0~\forall~i<r
and the mean value theorem. This implies that

\displaystyle||\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\ell}(\delta\vee b)^{r}).

(S30)

Meanwhile, by (
S23
) and a similar derivation, we know both
||\widetilde{A}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}
and
||A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}
are of order
\delta^{\ell}
. These together imply that

\displaystyle I_{3}=\mathcal{O}(||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\delta^{2\ell}(\delta\vee b)^{r}).

(S31)

•

For
I_{4}
, again
||A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)||_{\infty}\asymp||A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)||_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}(\delta^{\ell})
. For the difference, we have

\displaystyle||A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)||_{\infty}

(S32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j}\bigg|\int_{u}K(u)\bigg[\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{j}y\big(f(u\delta+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})

\displaystyle-f(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})+f(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})\big)f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg]du\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j}\bigg|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{j}y\int_{u}K(u)\bigg[\big(f(u\delta+\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})\big)

\displaystyle-\big(f(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})\big)\bigg]f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}du\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{j}\bigg|\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{j}y\int_{u}K(u)\bigg[(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z}\big(f^{\prime}(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{\prime}(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})\big)

\displaystyle+((\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*})^{T}\bm{z})^{2}\big(f^{\prime\prime}(u\delta+\widetilde{\bm{\beta}}^{T}\bm{z})-f^{\prime\prime}(\breve{\bm{\beta}}^{T}\bm{z})\big)\bigg]duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle M_{n}||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1}\delta^{\ell}+M_{n}^{2}||\widehat{\bm{\beta}}-\bm{\beta}^{*}||_{1}^{2},

where the last step follows from an
\ell
order Taylor expansion of
f^{\prime}(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})-f^{\prime}(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}|y,\bm{z})
for the first term and boundedness of
f^{\prime\prime}
for the second term.
This implies that

\displaystyle||A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)||_{\infty}\lesssim M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)(\delta^{\ell}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n))

(S33)

with probability approaching 1 by Assumption
6
. Therefore, we can obtain

\displaystyle I_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|(\bm{v}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-\bm{v}^{*T}A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta))(\bm{v}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)+\bm{v}^{*T}A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta))\bigg|

(S34)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|\bm{v}^{(1)T}(A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)-A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta))+(\bm{v}^{(1)T}-\bm{v}^{*T})A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)\bigg|\bigg|\bm{v}^{(1)T}A(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)+\bm{v}^{*T}A(\bm{\beta}^{*},\delta)\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\delta^{2\ell}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)(1\vee M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)/\delta^{\ell})+\eta_{2}(n)).

Combining all bounds for
I_{1},\dotso,I_{4}
, we know that uniformly over all
\delta\in\Delta

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg(\widehat{\bm{v}}^{(1)T}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{1}|}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{1}}A_{i}(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)},\delta)\bigg)^{2}-SB(\delta)\bigg|

(S35)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\delta^{2\ell}\bigg(\frac{\log(nd)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}}+(\delta\vee b)^{r}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)(1\vee\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta^{\ell}})+\eta_{2}(n)\bigg).

This completes the proof.

S9.2.3
Proof of Theorem
S6

By Lemma
S12
and Lemma
S13
, uniformly over all
\delta\in\Delta

\displaystyle|\widehat{M}(\delta)-M(\delta)|

(S36)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{n\delta}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\eta_{2}(n)+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{n\delta}}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)\bigg)

\displaystyle+||\bm{v}^{*}||_{1}^{2}\delta^{2\ell}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\frac{\log(nd)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nd)}{nb^{2\ell+1}}}+(\delta\vee b)^{r}+M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)(1\vee\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta^{\ell}})+\eta_{2}(n)\bigg)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n\delta}\psi_{1}(n)+\delta^{2\ell}\psi_{2}(n).

Recall that
M(\delta)=\frac{1}{n}V(\delta)+\frac{n-1}{n}SB(\delta),
where

\displaystyle V(\delta)=\frac{1}{\delta}(\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}\bm{v}^{*})(1+o(1))~~~~~\text{and}~~{SB(\delta)}=\delta^{2\ell}(\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{b}^{*})^{2}(1+o(1)).

(S37)

This implies that
\delta^{*}=(\frac{1}{n}\frac{\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{\Sigma}^{*}\bm{v}^{*}}{2\ell(\bm{v}^{*T}\bm{b}^{*})^{2}})^{1/(2\ell+1)}(1+o(1))
. By Assumption
5
we know
M(\delta)\gtrsim\frac{1}{n\delta}+\delta^{2\ell}
, which implies

\frac{|\widehat{M}(\delta)-M(\delta)|}{M(\delta)}\lesssim\psi_{1}(n)\vee\psi_{2}(n)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

uniformly over all
\delta\in\Delta
. That means for any
\epsilon>0
, uniformly over all
\delta\in\Delta
, the event
(1-\epsilon)M(\delta)\leq\widehat{M}(\delta)\leq(1+\epsilon)M(\delta)
holds with probability tending to 1. Under this event, we have that

\displaystyle M(\widehat{\delta})

\displaystyle=\widehat{M}(\widehat{\delta})+[M(\widehat{\delta})-\widehat{M}(\widehat{\delta})]

(S38)

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{M}(\delta^{*})+[M(\widehat{\delta})-\widehat{M}(\widehat{\delta})]

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon)M(\delta^{*})+\epsilon M(\widehat{\delta}),

where the first inequality holds because
\widehat{\delta}
is the minimizer of
\widehat{M}(\delta)
and the second inequality follows from
\widehat{M}(\delta^{*})\leq(1+\epsilon)M(\delta^{*})
and
\widehat{M}(\widehat{\delta})\geq(1-\epsilon)M(\widehat{\delta})
. As a result, we obtain
M(\widehat{\delta})\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}M(\delta^{*})
, from which we can claim that
\widehat{\delta}\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta^{*}
. To see this, let us consider the complement case
\widehat{\delta}>\frac{3}{2}\delta^{*}
. When it holds, we know from (
S37
) that

M(\widehat{\delta})\geq(\frac{3}{2})^{2\ell}SB(\delta^{*})(1+o(1))=(\frac{3}{2})^{2\ell}\frac{1}{2\ell+1}M(\delta^{*})(1+o(1))\geq 1.01M(\delta^{*})(1+o(1)),

where the second step follows from
M(\delta^{*})=(2\ell+1)SB(\delta^{*})(1+o(1))
and last step holds by the condition
\ell\geq 2
. However, the above inequality contradicts with
M(\widehat{\delta})\leq\frac{1+\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}M(\delta^{*})
for some sufficiently small
\epsilon
. This justifies the statement
\widehat{\delta}\leq\frac{3}{2}\delta^{*}
. Following a similar argument, we can show that
\widehat{\delta}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta^{*}
. Thus, with probability tending to 1, we have
|\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}|/\delta^{*}\leq 1/2
.

By definition, we have
M^{\prime}(\delta^{*})=0
and
\widehat{M}^{\prime}(\widehat{\delta})=0
. This implies that

\displaystyle\widehat{M}^{\prime}(\widehat{\delta})-M^{\prime}(\widehat{\delta})=-M^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\delta})(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}),

(S39)

for some intermediate value
\widetilde{\delta}
, which gives

\displaystyle(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*})=\frac{\widehat{M}^{\prime}(\widehat{\delta})-M^{\prime}(\widehat{\delta})}{-M^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{\delta})}.

(S40)

For any
{\delta}\asymp\delta^{*}
, after some algebra, we can show that

\displaystyle M^{\prime\prime}(\delta)\asymp\frac{1}{\delta^{3}n}.

(S41)

Similar to the proof of (
S36
), we can show that
(\widehat{M}^{\prime}-M^{\prime})(\delta)
has the same order as
(\widehat{M}-M)(\delta)
, except for an additional factor
\frac{1}{\delta}
. Since
|\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}|/\delta^{*}\leq 1/2
holds in probability, it implies
\widehat{\delta}
and
\widetilde{\delta}
are of the same order of
\delta^{*}
. Thus, (
S40
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}}{\delta^{*}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\psi_{1}(n)\vee\psi_{2}(n).

(S42)

This completes the proof.

S9.2.4
Proof of Theorem
S7

We start from the following two lemmas.

Lemma S14
.

Denote
s(\delta)=\sqrt{\delta}\bm{v}^{T}\nabla R_{\delta}^{n}(\bm{\beta})
for some fix
\bm{\beta}
and
\bm{v}
, under the conditions of Theorem
S7
we have

\sqrt{n}\left|s(\widehat{\delta})-s(\delta^{*})-s^{\prime}(\delta^{*})(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*})\right|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Proof.

Note that

s(\delta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}w(y_{i})y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{T}\bm{v}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta}}K\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{\delta}\right),

s^{\prime}(\delta)=\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}w(y_{i})y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{T}\bm{v}\left[-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{-\frac{3}{2}}K\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{\delta}\right)-(x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}})\delta^{-\frac{5}{2}}K^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{\delta}\right)\right].

With some algebra we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left|s(\widehat{\delta})-s(\delta^{*})-s^{\prime}(\delta^{*})(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}w(y_{i})y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{T}\bm{v}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}\Bigg[(x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}})^{2}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3(x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}})K^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{7}{2}}+\frac{3}{4}K\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{5}{2}}\Bigg](\widehat{\delta}-t)dt\Bigg|

(S43)

Now consider the first term in the integral in (
S43
).
Denote

G_{i}=w(y_{i})\bm{z}_{i}y_{i}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}(x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}})^{2}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dt

and
G_{im},1\leq m\leq d
as its coordinate. Consider event
A\coloneqq\{\frac{\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}}{\delta^{*}}\lesssim C_{n,\delta*}\}
, where
C_{n,\delta*}=\psi_{1}\left(n,\delta^{*}\right)\vee\psi_{2}\left(n,\delta^{*}\right)
. For each
m
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{z}z_{m}y\int_{x}(x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dtf(x\mid y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}=u\delta^{*}\atop=

\displaystyle(\delta^{*})^{3}\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{z}z_{m}y\int_{u}u^{2}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{u\delta^{*}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dtf(u\delta^{*}+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z})duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}.

Note that
\frac{\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*}}{\delta^{*}}\coloneqq C_{n,\delta^{*}}=o(1)
, hence

\displaystyle\int_{u}u^{2}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{u\delta^{*}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dtf(u\delta^{*}+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z})du

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{*-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*})^{2}\int_{u}u^{2}K^{\prime\prime}\left(u(1+o(1))\right)\left(f(\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z})+u\delta^{*}f^{\prime}(\tau u\delta^{*}+\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z})\right)du,

where
\tau\in[0,1]
. Since
\int u^{2}|K^{\prime\prime}(u)|du,\int|u^{3}||K^{\prime\prime}(u)|du
are bounded by a constant,
the last formula is bounded by
C\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}
for some constant
C
.
This implies that
\mathbb{E}\left[G_{im}\mid A\right]\lesssim\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}
.

Then we look at its variance. Similarly, note that
\int u^{4}K^{\prime\prime}(u)^{2}du,\int|u^{5}|K^{\prime\prime}(u)^{2}du
are bounded by a constant, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G_{im}^{2}\mid A]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)^{2}\int_{z}z_{m}^{2}\int_{x}(x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z})^{4}\left(\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dt\right)^{2}f(x\mid y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\delta^{*})^{5}(\delta^{*-\frac{5}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2})^{2}=C_{n,\delta*}^{4}.

Also we know for each
m=1,\ldots,d
,

\left|z_{m}y\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}(x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z})^{2}K^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{9}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dt\right|\lesssim M_{n}^{3}\delta^{*-\frac{5}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}.

Therefore, applying Bernstein inequality with
M_{n}^{3}\delta^{*-\frac{5}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we have for some constant
C
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{im}\right|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}\mid A\right)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-1}\right),

which further implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{im}\right|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{im}\right|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}\mid A\right)+\mathbb{P}(A^{c})=o(1).

Hence

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}G_{im}\right\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}).

(S44)

Similarly, denote

H_{i}=w(y_{i})\bm{z}_{i}y_{i}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}(x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}})K^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{7}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dt,

J_{i}=w(y_{i})\bm{z}_{i}y_{i}\int_{\delta^{*}}^{\widehat{\delta}}K\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z_{i}}}{t}\right)t^{-\frac{5}{2}}(\widehat{\delta}-t)dt

and
H_{im},J_{im},1\leq m\leq d
as their coordinate. Note that for
H_{im}
, we have

\int|u||K^{\prime}(u)|du,\int u^{2}|K^{\prime}(u)|du,\int u^{2}K^{\prime}(u)^{2}du\int|u^{3}|K^{\prime}(u)^{2}du

are bounded, for
J_{im}
, we have
\int|K(u)|du,\int|u||K(u)|du,\int K(u)^{2}du,\int|u|K(u)^{2}du
are bounded,
it can be shown that

\mathbb{E}\left[H_{im}\mid A\right]\lesssim\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[J_{im}\mid A\right]\lesssim\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}[H_{im}^{2}\mid A]\lesssim C_{n,\delta*}^{4}
,
\mathbb{E}[J_{im}^{2}\mid A]\lesssim C_{n,\delta*}^{4}
,
|H_{im}|\lesssim M_{n}^{2}\delta^{*-\frac{3}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}
and
|J_{im}|\lesssim M_{n}\delta^{*-\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}
.
Therefore, applying Bernstein inequality with
M_{n}^{2}\delta^{*-\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
M_{n}\delta^{*-\frac{1}{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we have for some constant
C
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H_{im}-\mathbb{E}H_{im}\right|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}\mid A\right)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-1}\right),

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}J_{im}-\mathbb{E}J_{im}\right|>C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}\mid A\right)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-1}\right),

respectively. Hence

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}H_{im}\right\|_{\infty},\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}J_{im}\right\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}).

(S45)

Plugging (
S44
) and (
S45
) back to (
S43
) we have

\sqrt{n}\left|s(\widehat{\delta})-s(\delta^{*})-s^{\prime}(\delta^{*})(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*})\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n}\|\bm{v}\|_{1}\delta^{*\frac{1}{2}}C_{n,\delta*}^{2}).

Under the conditions of Theorem
S7
, the desired result holds.
∎

Lemma S15
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
S7
, we have

\sqrt{n\delta^{*}}(\widehat{\delta}-\delta^{*})\left|\bm{v}^{*T}\left(\nabla_{\cdot\delta}R_{\delta^{*}}^{n_{(j)}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\right)-\nabla_{\cdot\delta}R_{\delta^{*}}^{n_{(j)}}\left(\bm{\beta}^{*}\right)-\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\delta}R_{\delta^{*}}^{n_{(j)}}\left(\bm{\beta}^{*}\right)\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\right)\right)\right|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

for
(j,k)\in\{(1,2),(2,1)\}
.

Proof.

Note that

\nabla\bar{R}_{\delta}^{i}\left(\bm{\beta}\right)=w\left(y_{i}\right)\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}K\left(\frac{y_{i}\left(x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}_{i}\right)}{\delta}\right),

\nabla_{\cdot\delta}\bar{R}_{\delta}^{i}\left(\bm{\beta}\right)=\nabla_{\delta}(\nabla\bar{R}_{\delta}^{i}\left(\bm{\beta}\right))=-w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z_{i}}}{\delta^{2}}K\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}\right)-w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}K^{\prime}\left(\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}\right)\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}.

With some algebra we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{v}^{*T}\left(\nabla_{\cdot\delta}R_{\delta^{*}}^{n_{(j)}}\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}\right)-\nabla_{\cdot\delta}R_{\delta^{*}}^{n_{(j)}}\left(\bm{\beta}^{*}\right)-\nabla^{2}_{\cdot\delta}R_{\delta^{*}}^{n_{(j)}}\left(\bm{\beta}^{*}\right)\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\right)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}3w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{T}\bm{v}^{*}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}^{T}\bm{v}^{*}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{v}^{*}\right\|_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}3w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{v}^{*}\right\|_{1}\left\|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right\|_{\infty}

(S46)

Denote

G_{i}=w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt,

F_{i}=w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt,

and
G_{im},F_{im}
,
1\leq m\leq d
as its coordinates. Consider the event
A:=\left\{\left\|\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}^{*}\right\|_{1}\lesssim C\eta_{1}(n)\right\}
for some constant
C
. Denote
\Delta=\frac{\left(\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)}-\bm{\beta}\right)^{T}\bm{z}}{\delta}
, then following the similar proof in Lemma
S5
, we can show that
\mathbb{E}\left[G_{im}\mid A\right]\lesssim|\Delta|^{2}\asymp\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}}
, and
\mathbb{E}\left[G_{im}^{2}\mid A\right]\lesssim\frac{M_{n}^{4}\eta_{1}(n)^{4}}{\delta^{7}}
. Also we know for each
i\in\mathcal{N}_{j},m=1,\ldots,d
,
|G_{im}|\lesssim\frac{M_{n}}{\delta^{2}}\left(\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta}\right)^{2}
. Therefore, by Bernstein inequality with
M_{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{m}\right|>\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}\mid A\right)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-1}\right).

Then since the event
A
holds with probability tending to 1, we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}G_{im}-\mathbb{E}G_{m}\right|>\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta^{3}}}\right)=o(1),

and therefore the first term in (
S46
) follows

\left\|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}3w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime}(t)\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\right).

(S47)

Now we analyze
F_{i}
. Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}y}{\delta^{2}}\int_{x}\int_{\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t\left(\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}}{\delta}-t\right)dtf(x\mid y,\bm{z})dxf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle(u=\left(x-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\right)/\delta)\atop=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}y}{\delta}\int_{u}\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dtf\left(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}y}{\delta}\int_{u}\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dtf\left(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)\int_{\bm{z}}z_{m}y\int_{u}u\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dtf^{\prime}\left(\tau u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z},

(S48)

where
\tau\in[0,1]
.
Note that

\displaystyle\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dt=-u\Delta K^{\prime\prime}(u)+(u+\Delta)K^{\prime}(u+\Delta)+(\Delta-u)K^{\prime}(u)-2K(u+\Delta)+2K(u).

Since
K^{\prime}
degenerates at the boundaries and
\int K(u)du=1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{u}\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dtf\left(\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)du=0.

Therefore, we only need to consider the second term of (
S48
).
Note that
|f^{\prime}(x\mid y,\bm{z})|
is bounded, we have for some constant
C,0<\tau<1
,

\displaystyle\int_{u}u\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dtf^{\prime}\left(\tau u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)du

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C\int_{t}|tK^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)|\int_{t-\Delta}^{t}|u(u+\Delta-t)|dudt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1-\tau)\Delta^{2}\int_{t}\left|tK^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)(t-\tau\Delta)\right|dt.

Note that
\int|t||K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)|dt,\int t^{2}|K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)|dt
are bounded, hence

\mathbb{E}\left[F_{im}\mid A\right]\lesssim\Delta^{2}\lesssim\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}}
.
For the second moment, note that
\int u^{2}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)^{2}du,\int|u^{3}|K^{\prime\prime\prime}(u)^{2}du
are bounded,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(w(y)\frac{yz_{m}}{\delta^{2}}\int^{\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}}{\delta}}_{\frac{x-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t\left(\frac{x-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right)^{2}\mid A\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)^{2}\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}^{2}}{\delta^{3}}\int_{u}\left(\int_{u}^{u+\Delta}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t(u+\Delta-t)dt\right)^{2}f\left(u\delta+\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}\mid y,\bm{z}\right)duf(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{y}w(y)^{2}\int_{\bm{z}}\frac{z_{m}^{2}}{\delta^{3}}\Delta^{4}f(y,\bm{z})d\bm{z}\lesssim\frac{M_{n}^{4}\eta_{1}(n)^{4}}{\delta^{7}}.

Note that
|F_{im}|\lesssim\frac{M_{n}}{\delta^{2}}\cdot\frac{M_{n}}{\delta}\left(\frac{M_{n}\eta_{1}(n)}{\delta}\right)^{2}=\frac{M_{n}^{4}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{5}}
. Therefore, by Bernstein inequality with
\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{\delta}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we can obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{m}\left|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}F_{im}-\mathbb{E}F_{m}\right|>\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{3}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{n\delta}}\mid A\right)\leq\mathcal{O}\left(d^{-1}\right),

which further implies that

\left\|\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}_{j}}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{N}_{j}}w(y_{i})\frac{y_{i}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\frac{x_{i}-\bm{\beta}^{*T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}^{\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}}K^{\prime\prime\prime}(t)t\left(\frac{x_{i}-\widehat{\bm{\beta}}^{(k)T}\bm{z}_{i}}{\delta}-t\right)dt\right\|_{\infty}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{M_{n}^{2}\eta_{1}(n)^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\right).

(S49)

Plugging (
S47
) and (
S49
) back to (
S46
) we have
